91.78706pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.83046pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}

∎

Examples 8.2
.

We consider our motivating examples of bilinear spaces and exponential fields (Examples
3.1
).

(1)

The join of two bilinear subspaces
A,B\subseteq V
is simply given by their span. So the forgetful functor
F:\operatorname{\bf Bil}_{K}\to\operatorname{\bf Vec}_{K}
preserves finite joins of subobjects and hence lifts base monotonicity.

(2)

The canonical independence relation
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{EA}
on
\operatorname{\bf EAF}
, which is the lift of
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\operatorname{td}}
along
F:\operatorname{\bf EAF}\to\operatorname{\bf ACF}
is known to not satisfy base monotonicity, so we know that
F
cannot preserve binary joins of subobjects. The failure of base monotonicity is spelled out in detail in
[
2
, Example 4.23]
. We will adjust that example to show how preserving binary joins fails.

Let
C
be any EA-field and let
K
be an algebraically closed field containing
C
as well as elements
a,b,d
that are algebraically independent over
C
. Set
A=C(a)^{\operatorname{alg}}
and
B=C(b)^{\operatorname{alg}}
, where
(-)^{\operatorname{alg}}
is the algebraic closure, and choose any exponential maps on them extending the one from
C
. Pick any
u\in C(a,b)^{\operatorname{alg}}
that is not in
A+B
(e.g.,
u=ab
). We can then extend those exponential maps from
A
and
B
to all of
K
such that
\exp(u)=d
. We now have that the join of
A
and
B
as algebraically closed subfields of
K
is
C(a,b)^{\operatorname{alg}}
, whereas their join as EA-subfields is
\langle A\cup B\rangle^{EA}_{K}
, which contains
d
and is thus bigger than
C(a,b)^{\operatorname{alg}}
. We conclude that
F
does not preserve binary joins.

We finish this section by discussing a way to compute joins of subobjects in categories of the form
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{M}}
, and consequently how these are preserved by left multiadjoints. For this we will use multipushouts. These were implicitly defined earlier when we discussed multicolimits (see before Corollary
4.11
), but as we will actually use them here we recall their precise definition.

Definition 8.3
.

Let
A\xleftarrow{f}C\xrightarrow{g}B
be a span of morphisms. A
multipushout
of this span consists of a set
\{A\xrightarrow{h_{i}}P_{i}\xleftarrow{k_{i}}B\}_{i\in I}
of cocones, such that for any cocone
A\xrightarrow{a}D\xleftarrow{b}B
there is a unique
i\in I
and a unique
u:P_{i}\to D
such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{i}}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{P_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{D}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k_{i}}
\scriptstyle{b}

We refer to each of the cocones in the set
\{A\xrightarrow{h_{i}}P_{i}\xleftarrow{k_{i}}B\}_{i\in I}
as an
instance
of the multipushout.

We adjust the well-known construction of joins of subobjects using pushouts to one using multipushouts, and relative to a factorisation system.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category with a factorisation system
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
, where
\mathcal{M}
consists of monomorphisms. Suppose we are given a commuting square

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{D}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}

with
a,b\in\mathcal{M}
such that the multipushout of
A\leftarrow C\to B
exists. Then we can compute the join
A\vee B
of the subobjects
A,B\leq D
in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{M}}
as follows. First, we let
P
be the instance of the multipushout that admits a morphism into
D
. Then
A\vee B
is the
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
-factorisation of this morphism
P\to D
:

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\vee B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{M}
is a composable and left-cancellable class of monomorphisms
(see
[
8
, Proposition 2.1.1]
), the morphism
A\to P\to A\vee B
is in
\mathcal{M}
, and so
A\leq A\vee B
as subobjects in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{M}}
. Similarly
B\leq A\vee B
. Now let
E\leq D
be a subobject in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{M}}
such that
A,B\leq E
. Then we have the following diagram in
\mathcal{C}
:

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}

Any path from
C
to
D
gives the same morphism, and so the square involving
C,A,B,E
commutes because
E\to D
is in
\mathcal{M}
and is thus a monomorphism. Let
P^{\prime}
be the instance of the multipushout of
A\leftarrow C\to B
admitting a morphism into
E
. Composing this morphism
P^{\prime}\to E
with
E\to D
gives a morphism into the cocone
A\xrightarrow{a}D\xleftarrow{b}B
, and so
P^{\prime}=P
.

So the morphism
P\to D
factors through
E
, and as
E\to D
is in
\mathcal{M}
with
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
being a factorisation system, we have that
A\vee B\to D
factors through
E
. By left-cancellability of
\mathcal{M}
, this factorisation
A\vee B\to E
must be in
\mathcal{M}
, establishing that
A\vee B\leq E
as subobjects of
D
in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{M}}
.
∎

Corollary 8.5
.

Let
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a left multiadjoint and let
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
be a factorisation system on
\mathcal{D}
with
\mathcal{M}
consisting of monomorphisms. Suppose that:

(1)

\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
have multipushouts;

(2)

(F^{-1}(\mathcal{E}),F^{-1}(\mathcal{M}))
is a factorisation system on
\mathcal{C}
;

(3)

F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})
consists of monomorphisms;

(4)

\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
satisfy the right Ore condition with respect to cospans in
F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})
(resp.
\mathcal{M}
), so for any cospan
A\to D\leftarrow B
of morphisms in
F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})
(resp.
\mathcal{M}
) there is a span
A\leftarrow C\to B
(not necessarily in
F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})
or
\mathcal{M}
) making the relevant square commute.

Then
\mathcal{C}_{F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
have binary joins of subobjects and the restriction
F:\mathcal{C}_{F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})}\to\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
preserves these joins.

In particular, if
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is an independence relation on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
satisfying base monotonicity, monotonicity and invariance then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
satisfies base monotonicity on
\mathcal{C}_{F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})}
.

Proof.

The fact that
\mathcal{C}_{F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
have binary joins follows from Lemma
8.4
. Here we use assumption (4) to find the
C
in that lemma for any cospan
A\to D\leftarrow B
of morphisms in
F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})
or
\mathcal{M}
.

The fact that
F:\mathcal{C}_{F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})}\to\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
preserves these binary joins then follows because
F
preserves all the parts in the computation of a binary join. That is,
F
sends a
(F^{-1}(\mathcal{E}),F^{-1}(\mathcal{M}))
-factorisation to a
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
-factorisation by definition, and left multiadjoints preserve connected multicolimits (see Corollary
4.11
).

The final claim follows immediately from Theorem
8.1
.
∎

The statement of Corollary
8.5
can be simplified by assuming
\mathcal{C}
to be locally multipresentable, which it often is in the intended applications. Then conditions (1) and (4) are automatic, as such categories are multicocomplete and have pullbacks. See also the example below.

Example 8.6
.

Similarly to Example
4.14
(2), we can consider the forgetful functor
U:\sigma\text{-}\operatorname{\bf Gra}\to\operatorname{\bf Gra}
, from the category of graphs with endomorphisms to the category of graphs, which are both locally presentable. As in
4.14
(2), this functor is faithful and left adjoint. Consider the factorisation system
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
= (surjections, graph embeddings) in
\operatorname{\bf Gra}
, and note that its pre-image under
U
gives the same factorisation system in
\sigma\text{-}\operatorname{\bf Gra}
. By Corollary
8.5
we thus have that the restriction
U:\sigma\text{-}\operatorname{\bf Gra}_{\mathcal{M}}\to\operatorname{\bf Gra}_{\mathcal{M}}
preserves binary joins of subobjects.

We also note that the restriction
U:\sigma\text{-}\operatorname{\bf Gra}_{\mathcal{M}}\to\operatorname{\bf Gra}_{\mathcal{M}}
does not admit
2
-completions, where the relevant independence relation on
\operatorname{\bf Gra}_{\mathcal{M}}
will be the pullback squares (see also Example
7.7
). Let
A=\{a_{1},a_{2}\}
be the
\sigma
-graph where the endomorphism swaps
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
, and let
B=\{b_{1},b_{2}\}
be defined analogously. Let
D=\{a_{1},a_{2},b_{1},b_{2}\}
, with an edge between
a_{1}
and
b_{1}
. Then
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{D}_{\emptyset}B
in
\operatorname{\bf Gra}_{\mathcal{M}}
. If
U
were to admit
2
-completions then we would get a commuting diagram of graph embeddings like below

\textstyle{U(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U(E)}
\textstyle{U(\emptyset)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}}
\textstyle{U(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

However, the endomorphism on
E
would then have to send
a_{1}
and
b_{1}
to
a_{2}
and
b_{2}
respectively, which is impossible as there is an edge between
a_{1}
and
b_{1}
but not between
a_{2}
and
b_{2}
.

9.
Lifting everything

Definition 9.1
.

A functor
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
between AECats is called a
reduct functor
if it is faithful and preserves directed colimits.

The intuition behind a reduct functor should be that of taking reducts, so it is some sort of forgetful functor. The forgetful functors in our motivating Examples
3.1
are of this form. More generally, when
\mathcal{C}
is a category of models of some theory
T
in a signature
\mathcal{L}
, then for any signature
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}
and for any theory
T^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
with
T\models T^{\prime}
, taking
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
-reducts yields a reduct functor
F:\operatorname{\bf Mod}(T)\to\operatorname{\bf Mod}(T^{\prime})
.

Theorem 9.2
.

Suppose that
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is a reduct functor that admits
2
-completions. Let
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
be an independence relation on
\mathcal{D}
.

(1)

If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is stable and
F
reflects amalgamation of squares then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is stable.

(2)

If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is simple,
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
have binary joins of subobjects and
F
preserves those joins and either
F
has
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
-independent horn amalgamation or
F
admits
3
-completions then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is simple.

(3)

If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is NSOP
1
-like and
F
has
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
-independent horn amalgamation then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is NSOP
1
-like.

(4)

If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is NSOP
1
-like with strong
3
-amalgamation and
F
admits
3
-completions then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is NSOP
1
-like with strong
3
-amalgamation.

Proof.

We put results together about which properties lift under which assumptions. The properties invariance, monotonicity, transitivity and symmetry all lift along any functor, by Proposition
3.3
. Being a reduct functor allows us to apply Theorem
3.4
and conclude that the properties union and accessibility lift. The existence property lifts by Theorem
5.7
, because
F
admits
2
-completions.

Then for stable independence we only need to lift uniqueness, so we apply Proposition
4.3
.

For simple independence we need to lift both
3
-amalgamation and base monotonicity. The latter follows from Theorem
8.1
. The former follows from Theorem
7.8
if
F
has
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
-independent horn amalgamation. If
F
admits
3
-completions then we note that by Theorem
6.2

\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
satisfies strong
3
-amalgamation, and so we can apply Theorem
7.4
.

Finally, for NSOP
1
-like independence we only need to lift
3
-amalgamation. As before we either apply Theorem
7.8
, for case (3), or Theorem
7.4
, for case (4).
∎

In the above proof for case (4) we needed to assume strong
3
-amalgamation, as opposed to case (2). This is because Theorem
6.2
no longer applies as it requires base monotonicity, which is exactly the differentiating property between
simple and NSOP
1
-like independence.

Example 9.3
.

Theorem
9.2
applies to both forgetful functors
\operatorname{\bf Bil}_{K}\to\operatorname{\bf Vec}_{K}
and
\operatorname{\bf EAF}\to\operatorname{\bf ACF}
from Examples
3.1
. For the former, case (2) applies and for the latter both (3) and (4) apply (strong
3
-amalgamation is implicit in
[
10
, Theorem 6.5]
).

Theorem 9.4
.

Let
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a faithful left multiadjoint and let
\mathcal{M}
be a left-cancellable composable accessible and continuous class of monomorphisms in
\mathcal{D}
. Suppose that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is an independence relation on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
, that satisfies semi-invariance as an independence relation on
\mathcal{D}
.

(1)

If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is stable then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is stable.

(2)

If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is simple,
\mathcal{C}_{F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
have binary joins of subobjects and
F
preserves those then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is simple.

(3)

If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is NSOP
1
-like then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is NSOP
1
-like, and furthermore if
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
satisfies strong
3
-amalgamation then so does
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
.

Proof.

We put results together about which properties lift under which assumptions. The properties invariance, monotonicity, transitivity and symmetry all lift along any functor, by Proposition
3.3
. Left multiadjoints preserve connected colimits (Corollary
4.11
), and so in particular
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
preserves directed colimits. As
\mathcal{M}
is continuous, this implies that
F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})
is continuous and so
F:\mathcal{C}_{F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})}\to\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
preserves directed colimits. We can thus apply Theorem
3.4
to this restricted functor to see that union and accessibility are lifted. Existence is lifted by Theorem
5.3
.

For stability, uniqueness is lifted by Theorem
4.13
. Then for both simplicity and NSOP
1
-like independence, (strong)
3
-amalgamation is lifted by Theorem
7.1
.

That leaves us to show that base monotonicity lifts in case (2). For that, we wish to apply Theorem
8.1
, which requires
F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})
to consist of monomorphisms. This follows quickly from the fact that
\mathcal{M}
consists of monomorphisms and that
F
is faithful.
∎

Theorem 9.5
.

Let
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a faithful left multiadjoint between locally multipresentable categories, and let
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
be a factorisation system on
\mathcal{D}
with
\mathcal{M}
an accessible and continuous class of monomorphisms. Suppose that
(F^{-1}(\mathcal{E}),F^{-1}(\mathcal{M}))
is a factorisation system on
\mathcal{C}
. If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is a simple independence relation on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{M}}
, satisfying semi-invariance as an independence relation on
\mathcal{D}
, then
F^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is a simple independence relation on
\mathcal{C}_{F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})}
.

Proof.

We follow the proof of Theorem
9.4
, except we apply Corollary
8.5
instead to lift base monotonicity. This applies because assumptions (1) and (4) there are automatic for locally multipresentable categories and we assumed (2) to hold. Assumption (3), namely that
F^{-1}(\mathcal{M})
consists of monomorphisms, was also shown to hold in the proof of Theorem
9.4
.
∎

Example 9.6
.

We give an example, similar to Example
4.15
(2), that uses all the components of Theorem
9.5
. Let
\mathcal{C}
be the category of connected graphs with graph homomorphisms. Let
\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{\bf Gra}
and let
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})=(\text{surjections},\text{embeddings})
. Then
F
is a left multiadjoint. For a graph
G
, the family of connected components and their inclusions into
G
witness that
F
is left multiadjoint. Furthermore,
\mathcal{C}
is easily seen to be accessible and have all connected limits, so
\mathcal{C}
is locally multipresentable. Furthermore,
(F^{-1}(\mathcal{E}),F^{-1}(\mathcal{M}))=(\text{surjections},\text{embeddings})
. Let
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
be the independence relation on
\operatorname{\bf Gra}_{\operatorname{Emb}}
given by pullback squares. Then
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is a semi-invariant simple independence relation (semi-invariance is quickly verified and simplicity can for example be found in
[
16
, Theorem 1.2 and Example 4.11]
). So Theorem
9.5
applies and we conclude that the pullback squares form a simple independence relation on
\mathcal{C}_{\operatorname{Emb}}
. Finally, we note that
F
is not a left adjoint for the same reasons as in Example
4.15
(2).

Example 9.7
.

We now discuss a link to related work in
[
16
]
, where a category-theoretic construction of independence relations is given. We recall a simplified version of the main results in
[
16
]
, where we focus on regular monomorphisms rather than an arbitrary class
\mathcal{M}
of monomorphisms, but the current work applies to that generality as well.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is a locally finitely presentable category and that the class
\operatorname{Reg}
of regular monomorphisms is closed under composition. Then
[
16
, Theorem 1.1]
states that if
\operatorname{Reg}
is cubic (see
[
16
, Definition 3.8]
) then the pullback squares form an NSOP
1
-like independence relation on
\mathcal{C}_{\operatorname{Reg}}
. Further conditions are given for when this independence relation is simple (see
[
16
, Theorem 1.2]
) or stable (see
[
16
, Corollary 4.9]
and
[
21
, Theorem 3.1]
).

Let
M
be any monoid and consider the category
\mathcal{C}^{M}
of objects in
\mathcal{C}
with an
M
-action, which is locally presentable. There is the obvious forgetful functor
U:\mathcal{C}^{M}\to\mathcal{C}
. This functor is faithful and preserves both limits and colimits, so it has both adjoints (by
[
1
, Theorems 1.58 and 1.66]
and the Special Adjoint Functor Theorem). It also reflects limits (in fact, it is monadic), so
U^{-1}(\operatorname{Reg})
is the class of regular monomorphisms in
\mathcal{C}^{M}
. Furthermore, regular monomorphisms are always continuous and accessible (see the proof of
[
20
, Lemma 4.3]
). They are are also part of a factorisation system
(\mathcal{E},\operatorname{Reg})
, because they are composable (in essence this is
[
18
]
, and
[
16
, Fact 4.2]
gives directions on how to put it in the present terms). Here
\mathcal{E}
is the class of epimorphisms, which
U
preserves and reflects. In conclusion, Theorems
9.4
and
9.5
apply to
U
. The special case

\operatorname{\bf Set}^{M}
is considered in
[
25
]
.

So if
\operatorname{Reg}
is cubic then the pullback squares form an NSOP
1
-like independence relation on
\mathcal{C}_{\operatorname{Reg}}
, which is easily checked to satisfy semi-invariance as an independence relation on
\mathcal{C}
. Then
U^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
, i.e. the pullback squares in
\mathcal{C}^{M}_{\operatorname{Reg}}
, form an NSOP
1
-like independence relation on
\mathcal{C}^{M}_{\operatorname{Reg}}
. Furthermore, if
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}
is simple or stable then
U^{-1}(\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}})
is simple or stable respectively. Note that Example
4.14
(2) is a special case of this.
